Parties. This Amendment is made and entered into by and between the Wyoming
Department of Transportation (WYDOT), whose address is: 5300 Bishop Boulevard,
Cheyenne, WY 82009 and the City of Cheyenne Transit Program (Recipient) whose address
is: 322 West Lincolnway, Cheyenne, WY 82001.
Purpose of Amendment. This Amendment shall constitute the first amendment to the
Agreement between WYDOT and the Recipient. The purpose of this Amendment is to (a)
update the State Share/Local Share percentages to 100/0, and (b) replace "Attachment A"
with "Attachment A-l".
The original Agreement, dated November 14, 2022 required the Recipient to provide
transportation services to the general and special public (Project) for a total Agreement
amount of Three Hundred Thousand Dollars and no cents ($300,000.00) with an expiration
dale of September 30, 2023.
Term of the Amendment. This Amendment shall commence on December 27, 2022 or
upon the date the last required signature is affixed hereto, whichever is later (Effective
Date), and shall remain in full force and effect through the term of the Agreement, as

Amendments.

as follows:
"The basis for reimbursement shall be Net Operating Deficit (Total Operating
Expense - Farebox = Net Operatin2 Deficit); State One Hundred percent

B. As of the Effective Date of this Amendment, "Attachment A", Award Letter, which was
attached to the original Agreement, is superseded and replaced by "Attachment A-1",
Revised Award Letter, which is attached to this Amendment and incorporated into the
original Agreement by this reference. All references to "Attachment A" in the original
Agreement, and in any amendments thereto, are amended to read: "Attachment A-l".

Same Terms and Conditions. With the exception of items explicitly delineated in
this Amendment, all terms and conditions of the original Agreement, and any
previous amendments, between WYDOT and the Recipient, including but not
limited to sovereign immunity, shall remain unchanged and in full force and effect.
B. 
Counterparts. This Amendment may be executed in counterparts. Each
counterpart, when executed and delivered, shall be deemed an original and all
counterparts together shall constitute one and the same Amendment. Delivery by the
Recipient of an originally signed counterpart of this Amendment by facsimile or
PDF shall be followed up immediately by delivery of the originally signed
counterpart to WYDOT.

Parties. The parlies to this Agreement are the Wyoming Department of Transportation
(WYDOT), whose address is 5300 Bishop Blvd., Cheyenne, WY 82009, and City of
Cheyenne Transit Program (Recipient), whose address is 322 West Lineolnway, Cheyenne,

Purpose. This is a subaward of federal financial assistance from the WYDOT to the
Recipient. The purpose of this Agreement is to provide the terms and conditions by which
the Recipient shall provide transportation services to the general and special public
(Project).
Pursuant to Wyo. Stat. §§ 24-15-101 and 24-15-102 WYDOT is authori/cd to distribute
these program funds in accordance with guidelines of the WYDOT State Management Plan
and the Master Agreement with the Federal Transit Administration (FTA), This
Agreement is also subject to all the Certifications and Assurances required by the FTA.
WYDOT and the Recipient desire to secure and utilize grant funds for the public
transportation needs in small urban and rural areas in the State of Wyoming.
Term of Agreement. This Agreement is effective when all parties have executed it
(Effective Date). The term of this Agreement is from October 1, 2022, or the Effective
Date, whichever is later (Term Start Date), through September 30, 2023. The Recipient's

4. 
Payment.
A. 
WYDOT agrees to reimburse the Recipient for the Project described in Section 2
above. The total reimbursement shall not exceed Three Hundred Thousand
Dollars and No Cents ($300,000.00), comprised of up to Three Hundred
Thousand Dollars and No Cents ($300,000.00) State Funding. The Recipient
agrees that it will provide from local and unrestricted Federal funds, monies in the
amount sutTicient together with the Grant to assure payment of the actual Project
costs. The Recipient's eligible costs incurred shall be reimbursed on an approved
WYDOT reimbursement request form. WYDOT reserves the right to request
support documentation for any and all submittals. WYDOT will reimburse those
eligible costs based on the program guidelines for the item categories and its
appropriate reimbursement ratio. Requests for reimbursement shall be submitted
monthly, but may not exceed quarterly submittals. Therefore, reimbursement
requests for expenses more than three months removed from the reimbursement
request submittal to WYDOT may not be processed for payment by WYDOT.
Final invoices for reimbursement will be accepted by WYDOT no later than forty-
five (45) days from the expiration date of the Agreement as outlined in Section 3
above. The basis for reimbursement shall be Net Operating Deficit (Total
Operating Expense - Farcbox = Net Operating Deficit): State Fifty-six and
Fifty-six hundredths percent (56.56%), Local Forty-three and Forty-four
hundredths percent (43.44%); Administration. Maintenance, and Capital;
State eighty percent (80%), Local Twenty percent (20%). The Recipient shall
initiate and prosecute to completion all actions necessary to enable the Recipient to
provide its share of the Project costs at or prior to the time that such funds are
needed to meet Project costs. Reimbursement shall be made within forty-five (45)
days afler submission of invoice pursuant to Wyo. Stal. § 16-6-602. The Receipt
shall submit invoices in sufficient detail to ensure that reimbursements may be
made in conformance with this Agreement.
B. 
No reimbursement shall be made for work performed before the Effective Date of
this Agreement. Should the Recipient fail to perform in a manner consistent with
the terms and conditions set forth in this Agreement, payment under this Agreement
may be withheld until such time as the Recipient performs its duties and
responsibilities to the satisfaction of WYDOT.
C. 
Except as otherwise provided in this Agreement, the Recipient shall pay all costs
and expenses, including travel, incurred by Recipient or on its behalf in connection

WYDOT will report the required information to the Federal funding

Recipients of vehicle Agreements. The FFATA requires any person or entity
receiving contract or grant funds directly from the federal govemment to report
certain information regarding those funds through a centralized website,
www.fsrs.gov. The law requires that vou provide your Data Universal Numbering

Responsibilities of the Parties.
A. 
Project Scope. The Recipient shall undertake and complete the Project in
accordance with this Agreement and as described in its application, which is
incorporated into this Agreement by this reference.
(i) 
Provide public transportation services throughout the community.
B. 
The Recipient shall abide by the terms found in Attachment A, which is attached
to and incorporated into this Agreement by this reference.
C. 
WYDOT agrees to reimburse the Recipient in accordance with Section 4 above.
D. 
Purchase of Project Equipment. The purchase of all Project equipment financed
in whole or in part pursuant to this Agreement shall be undertaken by WYDOT on
behalf of the Recipient or with prior WYDOT approval in accordance with the
procedures established by WYDOT. Purchases of over one thousand dollars
($ 1,000.00) using Federal or State funds will be subject to approval of WYDO T in

E. 
Title to Project Equipment. Title to Project Equipment shall be in the name of
the Recipient subject to the restrictions on use and disposition of the Project
Equipment set forth herein. The following notification shall be typed on the face
of the title document of each vehicle by the County Clerk: "WYDOT Grant -
Restricted." It is the responsibility of the Recipient to ensure the County Clerk is
notified of this requirement.
F. 
Use of Project Equipment. The Recipient agrees that the Project equipment,
and/or facilities, shall be used for the purpose for which it was acquired, as long as
it is needed in the Project. If the Project equipment is no longer needed, or cannot
be used for the intended purpose, prior to selling or otherwise disposing of the
Project equipment, the Recipient shall immediately notify WYDOT. Before selling
or otherwise disposing of the Project equipment, the Recipient must receive a
notification from WYDOT allowing the sale or disposal. The Recipient shall not
execute any lease, pledge, mortgage, lien, or other contract touching or affecting
the Federal interest in any Project equipment, nor shall it obligate itself in any other
manner, with any third parly with respect to the Project equipment, unless such
lease, pledge, mortgage, lien, contract, or other obligation is either in conformance
with the standards of FT A guidelines or is otherwise expressly authorized in writing
by WYDOT; nor shall the Recipient or any other organization that obtains facilities
or equipment through this Grant, by any act or omission of any kind, impair its
continuing control over the use of Project equipment. The Recipient shall maintain
satisfactory records with regard to the use of the property and submit to WYDOT
upon request such information as is required in order to assure compliance with this
section. The Recipient shall immediately notify WYDOT in ail cases where Project
equipment is used in a manner substantially different from that described in the
Project description. The Recipient shall maintain an amount and form satisfactory
to WYDOT such insurance or self-insurance as will be adequate to protect Project
equipment throughout the period of required use. During the period of contract
performance, the Recipient shall maintain the Project equipment at a high level of
cleanliness, safety and mechanical soundness. The Recipient shall maintain
equipment in accordance to their WYDOT approved Maintenance Plan adopted by
its Board or Governing Body and on file with WYDOT. WYDOT and the FTA
shall have the right to monitor maintenance records in order to confirm that proper
upkeep has been conducted pursuant to this section.
G. 
Prohibited Interest. No member, ollicer or employee of the Recipient during his
tenure or one year thereafter shall have any interest, direct or indirect, in this
Agreement or the proceeds thereof.
H. 
Restrictions, Prohibitions, Controls and Eabor Provisions

(i) 
Equal Employment Opportunity. In connection with carrying out of the
Project, the Recipient shall not discriminate against any employee or
applicant for employment because of race, color, age, creed, sex, national
origin or disability. The Recipient shall take affirmative action to ensure
that applicants are employed, and that employees are treated during
employment, without regard to their race, color, creed, sex, age, national
origin or disability. Such action shall include, but not be limited to, the
following: employment, upgrading, demotion or transfer, recruitment or
recruitment advertising, layoff or termination; rates of pay or other forms of
compensation; and selection for training, including apprenticeship.
(ii) 
Disadvantaecd Business Enterprise (DBE) Requirements.
1. Policy. 
It is the policy of WYDOT that disadvantaged business
enterprises, defined as Minority Business Enterprises and Woman
Business Enterprises, shall have the maximum opportunity to
participate in the performance of contracts financed in whole or in part
with Federal funds under this Agreement. Consequently, the DBE
requirements of 49 CFR Part 26 applies to this Agreement.
2. DBE Obligation. The Recipient or its contractor agrees to ensure that
disadvantaged business enterprises as defined in 49 CFR Part 26 have
the maximum opportunity to participate in the performance of contracts
and subcontracts financed in whole or in part with Federal funds
provided under this Agreement. In this regard, all Recipients shall take
all necessary and reasonable steps in accordance with 49 CFR Part 26
to ensure that DBF's have the maximum opportunity to compete for and
perform contracts. The Recipient and their contractors shall not
discriminate on the basis of race, color, national origin, or sex in the
award and performance of WYDOT assisted contracts.
(Hi) 
Title VI Civil Rights Act of 1964. The Recipient shall comply and shall
assure the compliance by contractors and subcontractors under this Project
with all the requirements imposed by Title VI of the Civil Rights Act of
1964 (49 U.S.C. 2000d), the Regulations of the Department of
Transportation (DOT) issued there under, 49 CFR Part 21, and the
Assurance by the Recipient pursuant thereto.
(iv) 
Compliance with Elderly and Disabled Regulations. The Recipient shall
comply with applicable regulations regarding transportation for Elderly and
Disabled person, set forth in 49 CFR Part 27 and the Americans with
Disabilities Act of 1990.

I. 
Motor Vehicle Safety
(i) 
Motor Vehicle Safety Standards. The motor vehicles will comply with the
Motor Vehicle Safety Standards and Pollution requirements as established
by the United States Department of Transportation.
(H) 
Motor Vehicle Requirements. When new motor vehicles are purchased,
there shall be obtained from each vendor a certification in writing that: "The
horsepower of the vehicle is adequate for the speed, range and terrain in
which it will be required to operate and also to meet the demands of all
auxiliary power equipment."
J. 
Nondiscrimination On The Basis of Disability - 504 Regulations
(i) 
The Recipient shall ensure to WYDOT that persons with disabilities and
groups representing them have had adequate notice of and opportunity to
comment on the present and proposed plans for providing transportation to
disabled persons unable to use general public service.
(ii) 
The Recipient must certify to WYDOT that special efforts are being made
to provide transportation to eligible disabled persons. This service must
be reasonable in cost, meet a significant fraction for such transportation
needs, and be provided within a reasonable time.
(iii) 
I'he Recipient is to submit a status report to WYDOT 
(updated every three
years). The report must include;
1. A description of the service to the disabled, as compared to the general
public service.
2. Copies or a summary of the comments received in response to the
opportunity to comment.
3. A statement of any plans to significantly modify the services.
4. A statement of the resources devoted to the disabled service.

K. 
Safety Jurisdiction. FTA requires that all Recipients operate their service in a safe
manner and not allow any conditions to develop that "create a serious hazard of
death or injury."
Safety Training Classes are a mandated requirement of the Safely Jurisdiction
clause. Driver Safety and Passenger Assistance Training (TSI) or equal approved
training, ADA compliance training, and other technical training programs arc
required on a regular basis (as specified by the State Management Plan). The
Wyoming Public Transportation Association (WYTRANS) may charge reasonable
fees for the training. WYTRANS shall record all such program income, attendance
(names and agencies) and costs. WYTRANS shall maintain records and files of
this training information. The Recipient shall maintain independent employee
training records for their file as well. The Recipient shall notify WYTRANS of any
employee needed training to keep current with the requirements of this section. If
safety violations are found and a Recipient fails to correct them, or comply with the
training mandate, further financial assistance may be withheld.
L. 
Emergency Management. All Recipients arc required by WYDOT to cooperate
and coordinate with Emergency Management agencies in their area during natural
or man-made disasters. All FFA vehicles shall be used to transport people and/or
equipment as directed by WYDOT. All Recipients shall use their communication
equipment to insure timely evacuation of elderly and disabled people in their area.
M. 
Records and Reports
(i) 
Establishment and Maintenance of Accounting Records. The Recipient
shall establish and maintain, in accordance with requirements established
by WYDOT, separate accounts for the Project, cither independently or
within its existing accounting system, to be known as the Project Account.
(ii) 
Documentation of Project Costs. All charges to the Project Account shall
be supported by properly executed invoices, contracts or vouchers
evidencing in proper detail the nature and propriety of the charges, in
accordance with the rules and regulations of WYDOT. Copies and/or
supporting documentation of such invoices, contracts or vouchers may be
included with monthly billing statements to cover all charges submitted for
payment. The original documentation should be maintained by the
Recipient in the Project file and must be available to WYDOT upon request.
(iii) 
Checks. Orders and Vouchers. Any check or order drawn by the Recipient
with respect to any item which is or will be chargeable against the Project

(iv) 
Reports. The Recipient shall advise WYDOT regarding the progress of the
Project at such times and in such manner as WYDOT may require,
including, but not limited to meetings and interim reports. The Recipient

Recipient agrees to provide WYDOT with a copy of such audit report. The
Recipient shall keep records and audit reports on file for three years after
the close of their fiscal year.
6. 
Special Provisions
A. 
Assumption of Risk. The Recipient shall assume the risk of any loss of state or
federal funding, cither administrative or program dollars, due to the Recipient's
failure to comply with state or federal requirements. WYDOT shall notify the
Recipient of any state or federal determination of noncompliance.
B. 
Conflict of Interest. Per 2 CFR 200.112, the Recipient must disclose in writing
any potential conflict of interest to WYDOT including financial or other personal
interests.
C. 
Environmental Policy Acts. The Recipient agrees all activities under this
Agreement will comply with the Clean Air Act, the Clean Water Act, the National
Environmental Policy Act, and other related provisions of federal environmental
protection laws, rules or regulations.

MEMO 
To: 
Honorable Mayor Patrick Collins and Members of the City Council 
From: 
Jeanie Vetter, PLA, Parks and Greenway Planner  
 
Subject: Contract Modification #4, East Cheyenne Community Park Master Site 
Development Plan 
 
Date: 
April 18, 2023 
 
1. Parks and Greenway Planning has been working on plans to begin construction efforts at 
the Kiwanis Community Park, located at the SW corner of E. Pershing and Whitney Road.  
These plans include a detention pond/constructed wetlands/open water area that will 
provide all detention needs for future improvements to be made at the park as well as 
improved wildlife habitat and water quality within Dry Creek.  The project will include 
gravel walking paths surrounding the wetlands/detention area, two blinds for observing 
birds, and educational signage describing the Kiwanis Park Master Plan, 
wetland/riparian area wildlife, water quality and park history. 
2. The contract modification before you is for additional consultant fees relative to design 
revisions that were necessitated by permitting requirements of the Wyoming 
Department of Environmental Quality (WYDEQ), United States Army Corp of Engineers 
(USACE) and State of Wyoming Engineers Office.  The increased fees include 
environmental assessment to be performed by HDR for existing wetlands. The fees also 
include bidding and construction administration for construction of the project. 
3. With approval of this contract modification, it is expected that final construction 
documents can be completed, and approvals can be obtained from WYDEQ, USACE and 
the State Water Engineers office by the end of this year or early next year and 
construction can begin in the spring of 2024. 
4. WYDEQ has suggested that this project would be a good option for a 319 grant for water 
quality, available through their office. This grant would be a 60/40 match, with funding 
from $20,000.00 to $300,000.00 potentially available.  Kiwanis Club of Cheyenne will 
support this project with $80,000.00 in park sponsorship funds.  Cheyenne Audubon 
Society and Cheyenne Greenway Foundation have committed $5,500.00 to support the 
bird blind portion of the project. 
  
  
  
  
  
  
  
  Planning and Development Department 
2101 O’Neil Avenue, Suite 202, Cheyenne WY 82001 
(Phone) 307-637-6282 (Fax) 307-637-6366 

2 
 
5. Funding for this contract modification will come from the 2017 6th Penny Tax for the 
park. 
Thank you in advance for your consideration.  If you have any questions, please feel free to 
contact me. 
 
Respectfully, 
Jeanie Vetter

The above referenced contract is amended or modified as set forth in the following and these 
amendments and modifications of the plans and specifications are agreed to by the parties. All other 
aspects of the original agreement remain unchanged and in full force and effect. 
 
I. Description of Changes 
Revised Construction Documents, Permitting, Bidding, and Construction Administration for 
construction of gravel trails, interpretive signage and Dry Creek restoration/alignment analysis at 
the park.   
 
II. New Contract Items 
Add plan revisions, environmental assessment and permitting based on USACE, WYDEQ and 
State Engineers Office requirements. Add bidding and construction administration. 
 
III. Schedule  
Time of Performance shall change to “The Consultant shall perform its services as expeditiously 
as is consistent with such professional skill and care and the orderly progress of the Project.” 
Term and Termination date shall change to May 31, 2025

Re: East Cheyenne Park - Trail Plan, Interpretive Signage and Dry Creek Restoration - ADD SERVICE #1
Dear Jeanie,
In response to the recent on site meeting and on-going coordination with USACE and WYDEQ departments 
we are requesting an additional service for the subject project.  The additional work is listed below.
ADDITIONAL SCOPE ITEMS
Revised Wetland/Open Water Layout - Based on the requirements from the USACE, the existing natural 
feature will be required to remain in place.  Due to this requirement, the wetland/open water experience area 
will need to be revised.  RMS will colloborate with CivilWorx regarding this area - specifically in regards to the 
general shape and location.  
Verification of Master Plan impacts - Based on the new wetland area we would need to verify that this area 
will not impact the program to the east of the wetland area - specifically the planned soccer fields outlined in 
the master plan.  
Revised Construction Documents - Based on the new wetland area our construction documents will need 
to be revised based on these changes.  This includes trail realignment, relocation of signage and seeding/
planting plan revisions.
On-going Coordination - RMS will continue to coordinate and lead the project by organizing construction 
document submittals and scheduling any necessary meetings going forward.  This coordination time will also 
include coordinating with enviornmental consultant.
Meetings - Meetings w/ Client, team - allow 2
OPTIONAL - Bidding/Construction Administration - Russel+Mills Studios will provide Construction 
Administration services as related to the landscape construction Scope, as defined. Construction 
Administration services shall include Review testing agency reports; Review of contractor’s submittals and 
shop drawings;  site visits, submittal of field reports communicating construction activities to the Client; plant 
selection and tagging at the source location for major trees and specimen material; Substantial Completion 
and preparation of a final punch list; and final inspection at completion of punch list corrections. 
Construction administration will include (3) visits, that will include substantial and final walk(s).  Field reports 
provided to contractor and City for records. 
Additional Service Fee - Russell + Mills Studios	
	
	
	
	
	
	
Based on our previous agreement, we will bill these additional services on an hourly basis not to exceed 
$8,220.

City Contract No. ____________ 
 
PROFESSIONAL SERVICES AGREEMENT 
for 
MOSQUITO SITE TESTING AND MOSQUITO LARVICIDE 
APPLICATION 
 
This Professional Services Agreement (“Agreement”) is made and entered into by and 
between the City of Cheyenne, located at 2101 O’Neil Avenue, Cheyenne, Wyoming 82001 
(“City”), and Vector Disease Control International (VDCI), located at 1320 Brookwood Drive, 
Suite H, Little Rock, AR 72202 (“Consultant”).  This Agreement shall become effective upon 
the date of the last signature affixed hereto. 
 
RECITALS 
 
In consideration of the mutual covenants and obligations herein expressed, it is agreed by and 
between the parties as follows: 
 
1. 
SCOPE OF WORK 
The Consultant agrees to provide services for the Mosquito Site Testing and Mosquito 
Larvicide Application Project, in accordance with City of Cheyenne Invitation to Bid 
(ITB) #E-18-23, incorporated herein by reference, and the Consultant’s submitted bid 
proposal, incorporated herein by reference. 
 
2. 
TIME OF PERFORMANCE 
The work included in this Scope of Work will be completed by December 31, 2024. 
 
3. 
COMPENSATION 
In consideration of the services to be performed pursuant to this Agreement, the 
Consultant will bill the City and the City agrees to pay Consultant a not to exceed amount 
of One Hundred Eleven Thousand, Three Hundred Thirty-Four Dollars ($111,334.00) per 
year, for a two (2) year term total not to exceed Two Hundred Twenty-Two Thousand, 
Six Hundred Sixty-Eight Dollars ($222,668.00). 
 
4. 
CONSULTANT RESPONSIBILITY 
In providing services under this Agreement, the Consultant shall perform in a manner 
consistent with that degree of care and skill ordinarily exercised by members of the same 
profession currently practicing under similar circumstances. Upon notice to the 
Consultant, the Consultant will, without additional compensation, correct those services 
not meeting such a standard. 
 
5. 
COMPLIANCE WITH LAWS 
The Parties agree that they will perform their obligations as provided in this Agreement 
in accordance with all applicable laws and ordinances. 
 
 
3:29 pm, Apr 04 2023

City of Cheyenne / Professional Services Agreement 
Mosquito Site Testing and Mosquito Larvicide Application

6. 
DRUG-FREE WORKPLACE 
In compliance with the Drug-Free Workplace Act of November 1988, the City of 
Cheyenne has established an Alcohol and Controlled Substance Policy that pertains to 
alcohol and drug usage by City Employees. All architects, engineers, and other 
consultants under contract with City of Cheyenne, and their employees and sub-
consultants, are required to comply with the provisions of the City’s Alcohol and 
Controlled Substance Policy for drug and/or alcohol usage on City property or other sites 
occupied by the Consultant while performing the duties and responsibilities of the 
Agreement. It is the responsibility of the Consultant to familiarize themselves with the 
requirements of this policy and to inform all their employees and sub-consultants of the 
requirements and ensure their compliance. If the Consultant, their employees or sub-
consultants are found in violation of this policy, the Agreement may be terminated. 
 
7. 
INDEMNIFICATION/HOLD HARMLESS 
The Consultant agrees to indemnify, hold harmless, and defend the City from and against 
any and all liabilities, claims, penalties, forfeitures, and suits, and the cost and expenses 
incident thereto, including reasonable attorney’s fees, which may hereafter arise as a 
result the performance of the Consultant’s duties, including death or bodily injury to any 
person, destruction or damage to any property, contamination of or adverse effects on the 
environment, or any violation of governmental laws, regulations, or orders to the extent 
caused by (1) the Consultant’s breach of any term or provision of this Agreement; or (2) 
any negligent or wrongful act, error, or omission by the Consultant, or its employees or 
sub-consultants in the performance of this Agreement.  The Consultant acknowledges 
that it may incur a financial obligation to the City pursuant to the terms of this paragraph. 
 
8. 
INSURANCE REQUIREMENTS 
The Consultant must provide proof of the following insurance coverages: 
 
 
Commercial General Liability Insurance 
For claims arising out of bodily injury, illness, or death, or from damage to or destruction 
of property of others, including loss or use thereof, with minimum limits of $1,000,000 
per occurrence, $2,000,000 aggregate for the entire term of the Agreement. 
 
 
Workers’ Compensation 
Workers’ compensation coverage shall be in effect for the entire term of the Agreement, 
as required by Wyoming law, for all employees or agents providing services under this 
Agreement.  Consultant shall provide the City with proof of workers’ compensation or 
employer’s liability insurance coverage. 
  
 
If this coverage is provided pursuant to a “claims made” policy: 
 
 
a. 
Consultant shall, concurrently with the execution of this Agreement, 
provide the City with a certificate of insurance demonstrating that such 
coverage is or shall be in effect at the time the Consultant begins the 
provision of services under this Agreement; and 
 
 
b. 
In the event the Consultant’s services extend into a future policy period, 
the Consultant shall, prior to the policy expiration date, provide the City 
with a new certificate of insurance demonstrating that such coverage is or

Mosquito Site Testing and Mosquito Larvicide Application

shall be in effect during all periods of time that Consultant will provide 
services under this Agreement; and 
 
 
c. 
Consultant shall maintain said “claims made” coverage for a period of five 
(5) years following the last date that Consultant has provided services 
under this Agreement; and 
 
 
d. 
In the event the Consultant or the insurer terminates “claims made” 
coverage prior to the expiration of the periods provided in subparagraphs 
a., b., or c. of this paragraph, the Consultant shall provide to the City 
advance written notification of the termination of said coverage and shall 
provide the City with an endorsement for an extended reporting period 
(“tail coverage”) which shall be in effect for a period of time not less than 
five (5) years following the last date that Consultant has provided services 
under this Agreement.  
 
 
Additional Insurance Information 
 
The Consultant shall name the City of Cheyenne as an Additional Insured by 
endorsement on its insurance policies and shall provide the City with a copy of the 
endorsements.  This requirement does not apply to workers’ compensation and 
professional liability insurance policies. 
 
Consultant shall provide the City with certificates of insurance acknowledging the above-
stated coverages prior to beginning any work under this Agreement. 
 
It is understood and agreed that these policies are primary and not contributory. All 
policies required under this Agreement shall be in effect for the duration of the 
Agreement.  It shall be an affirmative obligation upon Consultant to immediately notify 
in writing the city risk manager, city clerk, and city attorney of any fact, circumstance, or 
occurrence that has resulted in or may result in the cancellation of, or substantive change 
to any insurance coverage required by this Agreement, and failure to do so shall be 
construed to be a breach of this Agreement. 
 
If requested by the City, the Consultant shall provide the City with copies of insurance 
policies and/or policy endorsements listing the City of Cheyenne as an additional insured.  
The City’s failure to request or review such policies, endorsements, or certificates shall 
not affect the City’s rights or Consultant’s obligation hereunder. 
 
Any insurance company providing coverage under this Agreement shall have a minimum 
A. M. Best rating of A- (excellent). 
 
9. 
MINORITY AND DISADVANTAGED BUSINESS ENTERPRISES 49 C.F.R. Part 26 
All parties to this Agreement assure that no person will be excluded from participation in, 
denied the benefits of, or otherwise discriminated against, in connection with the award 
and performance of this Agreement on the grounds of age, race, color, disability, national 
origin, or sex.

10. 
MONTHLY REPORT 
With every monthly billing, Consultant shall provide the City Representative with a 
written statement of the status of the work with respect to the Scope of Work, time sheets, 
and work schedule. Failure to provide the required monthly report will delay processing 
of any payment request until the report is submitted. 
 
11. 
INDEPENDENT CONSULTANT 
The Consultant shall function as an independent consultant for the purposes of this 
Agreement. The Consultant shall assume sole responsibility for any debts or liabilities 
that may be incurred by the Consultant in fulfilling the terms of this Agreement. Nothing 
in this Agreement shall be interpreted as authorizing the Consultant or its agents or 
employees to act as an agent or representative of or on behalf of the City or to incur any 
obligation of any kind on the behalf of the City. 
 
12. 
TAXES 
The Consultant agrees to pay all valid taxes, excises, license fees, permit fees, bills, 
debts, and obligations incurred by and in connection with its operations under this 
Agreement. 
 
13. 
DEFAULT 
Each and every term and condition in this Agreement shall be deemed to be a material 
element of the Agreement. In the event either party should fail or refuse to perform 
according to the terms of this Agreement, such party may be declared in default. 
 
14. 
REMEDIES 
In the event a party has been declared in default, such defaulting party shall be allowed a 
period of fifteen (15) days within which to cure the default.  In the event the default 
remains uncorrected, the non-defaulting party declaring default may elect to: 
 
 
a. 
Terminate the Agreement and seek damages, which damages shall not 
exceed the contract amount; or 
 
 
b. 
Treat the Agreement as continuing and require specific performance. 
 
15. 
TERM AND TERMINATION 
The term of this Agreement shall be from date of execution to December 31, 2024.  The 
parties may by mutual written agreement renew or extend this Agreement. 
The City may, without cause, and upon thirty days’ written notice to the Consultant, 
terminate this Agreement in whole or in part at any time, for the City’s convenience. 
Upon receipt of such notice, the Consultant shall: 
 
 
a. 
Discontinue all services affected; and 
 
 
b. 
Deliver to the City within five (5) days all data, drawings, specifications, 
reports, estimates, summaries, and such other information and materials as 
may have been accumulated by the Consultant in performing this 
Agreement, whether completed or in process. 
 
 
c. 
In the event of termination for convenience, the City will pay the 
Consultant for accepted work done and expenses incurred to the date of 
termination. Such acceptance shall not be unreasonably withheld. 
 

City of Cheyenne / Professional Services Agreement 
Mosquito Site Testing and Mosquito Larvicide Application

16. 
ADDITIONAL REMEDIES 
In the event the Consultant fails to strictly perform in accordance with this Agreement, 
the City may elect to correct the deficiencies and charge the Consultant. In the event of 
default of any of the conditions by either party which shall require the party not in default 
to commence legal or equitable action against the defaulting party each party shall bear 
its own costs and expenses, including without limitation, attorney’s fees.  
 
17. 
GOVERNING LAW, JURISDICTION AND VENUE 
The construction, interpretation, and enforcement of this Agreement shall be governed by 
the laws of the State of Wyoming.  The courts of the State of Wyoming shall have 
jurisdiction over any action arising out of this Agreement and over the parties, and the 
venue shall be the First Judicial District, Laramie County, Wyoming. 
 
18. 
GOVERNMENTAL IMMUNITY 
The City of Cheyenne, along with its officials and employees, does not waive its 
governmental immunity by entering into this Agreement, except to the extent necessary 
for the parties to pursue a contract action to clarify or enforce the written terms of the 
Agreement.  Furthermore, the City specifically retains all immunities and defenses 
available to it as a sovereign or governmental entity pursuant to Wyo. Stat. § 1-39-101, et 
seq., and all other relevant state and federal law. Designations of venue, choice of law, 
enforcement actions, and similar provisions should not be constructed as a waiver of 
governmental immunity. The parties agree that any ambiguity in this Agreement shall not 
be strictly construed, either against or for either party, except that any ambiguity as to 
governmental or sovereign immunity shall be construed in favor of governmental 
immunity. 
 
19. 
MONITORING ACTIVITIES 
The City shall have the right to monitor all activities related to this Agreement that are 
performed by the Consultant or its sub-consultants. This shall include, but not be limited 
to, the right to make site inspections at any time and with reasonable notice; to bring 
experts and consultants on site to examine or evaluate completed work or work in 
progress; to examine the books, ledgers, documents, papers, and records pertinent to this 
Agreement; and to observe personnel in every phase of performance of the related work. 
 
20. 
OWNERSHIP OF DOCUMENTS AND WORK PRODUCT 
All deliverables by the Consultant, all work product of the Consultant, all notes, 
calculations, memoranda, or any other documentation generated by the Consultant 
pursuant to the scope of this Agreement, shall be the property of the City of Cheyenne 
and shall be provided to the City of Cheyenne immediately upon request. 
 
21. 
NON-DISCRIMINATION 
The Consultant shall comply with the Civil Rights Act of 1964, the Wyoming Fair 
Employment Practices Act (Wyo. Stat. § 27-9-105, et seq.), the Americans With 
Disabilities Act (ADA), as amended, 42 U.S.C. § 12101, et seq., and the Age

and shall not discriminate against any individual on the grounds of age, sex, creed, color,

22. 
FORCE MAJEURE.

war, acts or threats of terrorism, civil disorder, unauthorized strikes, governmental

by the City.  The work shall be completed by October 1, 2023.  If the work has not been completed 
within the time stipulated above, including any extensions of time issued by the City for excusable 
delays, the Contractor and his/her sureties shall pay the City fixed, agreed liquidated damages, as 
stipulated in the Supplemental Conditions, for each calendar day of delay until the work is 
completed. 
 
ARTICLE 4.  COMPENSATION AND METHOD OF PAYMENT.  The CITY will pay 
the CONTRACTOR for the performance of the Contract in current funds, the sum of One 
Hundred Fifty-Three Thousand, One Hundred Fifty-Five Dollars ($153,155.00).  In the event 
there are changes in the estimated quantities shown on the Bid Proposal, the unit prices multiplied 
by the actual quantities shall govern, and the total contract amount will be adjusted accordingly.  
The City agrees to pay the above amount for contractual services in the following manner, upon 
receipt of appropriate documentation: 
 
a. 
The Contractor will be paid on a monthly basis for percentage of estimated 
work completed.  Submittal will be at least seven (7) business days prior to the payable due 
date as established annually by the City Treasurer’s Office.  The pay request shall be 
submitted on the Contract Payment Request Form and Itemized Pay Request or the AIA 
Documents G702 and G703.  The engineer will review the estimate for approval prior to 
payment. 
3:24 pm, Apr 04 2023

Page 2 of 2 
b. 
The City will withhold five percent (5%) of the dollar value of the work 
completed for a minimum of forty-one (41) calendar days after Notice of Final Settlement 
has been published in accordance with Wyo. Stat. §16-6-116.  Upon completion of the 
work under this Contract, the Contractor shall submit a Contractor’s Certificate of 
Completion; the Consent of Surety; Final Waivers of Lien from the Contractor, and all 
Sub-Contractors, Suppliers and Materialmen; Affidavit of Release of Liens; Affidavit of 
Payment; and a current Workers Compensation Certificate.  Final payment will not be 
made until the above documents have been received by the City and all items on the Punch 
List have been completed, and the advertising requirements have been met. 
 
ARTICLE 5.  CONTRACT.  The executed Contract Documents shall consist of the 
following: 
 
1. This Agreement;  
2. Addenda; 
3. Invitation for Bids; 
4. Instructions to Bidders; 
5. Signed Bid Proposal; 
 
6. General Conditions and Insurance; 
7. Supplemental Conditions; 
8. Part IV Forms & Notices; 
9. Specifications and Special 
Provisions; and  
10. Drawings. 
 
This Agreement, together with other documents enumerated in this Article 5, which said 
other documents are as fully a part of the Contract as if hereto attached or herein repeated, forms 
the Contract between the parties hereto. 
 
IN WITNESS WHEREOF, THAT the governing body of the City of Cheyenne has 
authorized the Mayor as Executive Officer of the City to enter into this Agreement, and that the

MUNICIPAL BUILDING, 2101 O'NEIL AVE, ROOM 309, CHEYENNE, WYOMING 82001
CITY OF CHEYENNE, PURCHASING MANAGER, PHONE: (307) 773-1045, EMAIL: TBARTTELBORT@CHEYENNECITY.ORG
INQUIRIES REGARDING THIS BID SHOULD BE DIRECTED TO:

Page 1 of 2 
CITY OF CHEYENNE, WYOMING 
AGREEMENT 
 
Bid No. S-21-23 
 
Contract No. _______ 
 
THIS AGREEMENT, entered into this ____ day of ___________, _____, by and between 
the CITY OF CHEYENNE, WYOMING, hereinafter referred to as the “CITY”, and Miller & 
Sons Construction, Inc., hereinafter referred to as the “CONTRACTOR”. 
 
WITNESSETH that the Contractor and the City, for the considerations stated herein, 
mutually agree as follows: 
 
ARTICLE 1.  STATEMENT OF WORK.  The Contractor shall furnish all supervision, 
technical personnel, labor, materials, machinery, tools, equipment, and services, including utility 
and transportation services, and perform and complete all work in an efficient and workmanlike 
manner in the construction of the “2023 Miscellaneous Concrete” project all in strict accordance 
with the Contract Documents including all addenda thereto, numbered and dated: Addendum One 
(1), dated April 12, 2023. 
 
ARTICLE 2.  RESPONSIBLE DESIGNEE FOR THE CITY.  The Contractor shall in any 
and all matters relating to the scope of services to be provided under this Contract or any other 
provisions herein, contact the City Engineer, or his/her designated representative. 
 
ARTICLE 3.  TIME FRAME FOR COMPLETION.  The services to be performed under 
this Agreement shall commence on the date stipulated in the “Notice to Proceed” that will be issued 
by the City.  The work shall be completed by October 31, 2023.  If the work has not been 
completed within the time stipulated above, including any extensions of time issued by the City 
for excusable delays, the Contractor and his/her sureties shall pay the City fixed, agreed liquidated 
damages, as stipulated in the Supplemental Conditions, for each calendar day of delay until the 
work is completed. 
 
ARTICLE 4.  COMPENSATION AND METHOD OF PAYMENT.  The CITY will pay 
the CONTRACTOR for the performance of the Contract in current funds, the sum of One Million, 
Thirteen Thousand, One Hundred Fifty Dollars and Ninety-Eight Cents ($1,013,150.98).  In 
the event there are changes in the estimated quantities shown on the Bid Proposal, the unit prices 
multiplied by the actual quantities shall govern, and the total contract amount will be adjusted 
accordingly.  The City agrees to pay the above amount for contractual services in the following 
manner, upon receipt of appropriate documentation: 
 
a. 
The Contractor will be paid on a monthly basis for percentage of estimated 
work completed.  Submittal will be at least seven (7) business days prior to the payable due 
date as established annually by the City Treasurer’s Office.  The pay request shall be 
submitted on the Contract Payment Request Form and Itemized Pay Request or the AIA 
Documents G702 and G703.  The engineer will review the estimate for approval prior to 
payment. 
8:15 pm, Apr 18 2023

Page 2 of 2 
b. 
The City will withhold five percent (5%) of the dollar value of the work 
completed for a minimum of forty-one (41) calendar days after Notice of Final Settlement 
has been published in accordance with Wyo. Stat. §16-6-116.  Upon completion of the 
work under this Contract, the Contractor shall submit a Contractor’s Certificate of 
Completion; the Consent of Surety; Final Waivers of Lien from the Contractor, and all 
Sub-Contractors, Suppliers and Materialmen; Affidavit of Release of Liens; Affidavit of 
Payment; and a current Workers Compensation Certificate.  Final payment will not be 
made until the above documents have been received by the City and all items on the Punch 
List have been completed, and the advertising requirements have been met. 
 
ARTICLE 5.  CONTRACT.  The executed Contract Documents shall consist of the 
following: 
 
1. This Agreement;  
2. Addenda; 
3. Invitation for Bids; 
4. Instructions to Bidders; 
5. Signed Bid Proposal; 
 
6. General Conditions and Insurance; 
7. Supplemental Conditions; 
8. Part IV Forms & Notices; 
9. Specifications and Special 
Provisions; and  
10. Drawings. 
 
This Agreement, together with other documents enumerated in this Article 5, which said 
other documents are as fully a part of the Contract as if hereto attached or herein repeated, forms 
the Contract between the parties hereto. 
 
IN WITNESS WHEREOF, THAT the governing body of the City of Cheyenne has 
authorized the Mayor as Executive Officer of the City to enter into this Agreement, and that the 
parties hereto have caused this Agreement to be executed on the day and year in the first part herein 
written.

MEMORANDUM 
TO: 
 
Mayor Patrick Collins, and City Council 
FROM: 
 
TJ Barttelbort, Purchasing Manager 
DATE: 
 
April 19, 2023 
SUBJECT: 
Consideration to Purchase between the City of Cheyenne, and High West Energy 
for installation of 3 Phase Power at the new Countryside & Whitney Fire Station 
______________________________________________ 
This is a Single Source purchase through High West Energy, therefore no bids were requested. 
High West Energy is the only electrical utility company capable of providing electrical service to 
the new Fire Station at Countryside & Whitney, therefore, the new 3 Phase Power must be installed 
by High West Energy. 
The total requested purchase amount is $120,000.00. 
Additional information is attached, provide by Chief Kopper. 
 
Funding: 
030-16-1640-41-43804 (2017 Special Purpose Option Tax) 
032-16-1640-41-43804 (2021 Special Purpose Option Tax) 




April 24 & May 8, 2023
May 1, 2023
4/19/2023
Apr 19 2023, 8:14 am

REQUEST TO PURCHASE OVER $35,000.00 
Consideration to Purchase between the City of Cheyenne, and High West Energy, for installation 
of 3 Phase Power at the new Countryside & Whitney Fire Station.  This is a single source 
purchase, therefore no bids were requested.  The total requested amount is $120,000.00. 
 
This will be reviewed by the City Council Finance Committee on May 1, 2023 and voted on at 
the May 8, 2023 City Council meeting. 
 
TJ Barttelbort, Purchasing Manager 
City of Cheyenne, Wyoming 
(307) 773-1045 
 
Published in:  Wyoming Tribune-Eagle 
Dates: April 21 & 28, 2023

ACE’S RANGE LLC
1505 Pioneer Ave 1(307)631-71811 acesrangewygmai1.com
The Honorable Patrick Collins and Members of the Cheyenne City Council
Municipal Building
Mayor’s Office Room 310
2101 O’Neil Avenue
Cheyenne, WY 82001
RE: Application for Bar and Grill Entertainment carve out
Honorable Mayor Collins and Members of the Cheyenne City Council,
Ace’s Range is a new virtual simulation where popular outdoor activities are done inside with
virtual simulators. In our case, we propose utilizing both golfing and shooting state of the art
simulators. A similar venue, XGolf, has been open in Fort Collins since 2019 and brings in
visitors, sales tax and property tax. Equally important Ace’s Range fills the empty warehouse
space at 1 5th Street and Pioneer Avenue with another quality venue in downtown Cheyenne.
We respectfully request your consideration in granting our business the newly available bar and
grill entertainment carve out liquor license. It is imperative to this type of business that we have
the ability to serve alcohol to the extent only allowed with a bar and grill entertainment carve out
license.
We know you will be considering a lot of requests and need as much information as possible to
make an informed decision. The most salient topics we would like to briefly touch on in this
letter are as follows:
15th Street Renaissance: We share the Governing Body’s passion for revitalizing downtown and
believe in the importance of the 151h Street Renaissance to that mission. Ace’s Range will be
located at the corner of 5 Street and Pioneer in a 1940’s warehouse that is vacant. We will
completely rehabilitate the interior and exterior of the building and create a vibrant property at
this important corner. We intend to partner with local businesses in the vicinity as well as local
organizations in the city to maximize the use of our simulators and facility.
Economic Activity: Partnerships with downtown businesses, property tax, sales tax,
partnerships with golf organizations and shooting clubs. Ace’s Range will offer leagues and
special events to attract business to the downtown area. Ace’s Range, in accordance with
Laramie County Health regulations, would like to offer food from downtown restaurants to be
delivered by Ace’s Range. Ace’s Range would also work with the food trucks to create a

schedule to add even more variety to our food options. In addition to these options we will
provide some basic ready to eat dishes.
Business Plan: If granted this license, we have two phases to our business plan. Phase I is to
complete the interior and exterior rehabilitation of the building. We have assembled the
professionals necessary currently working on the building in compliance with regulations and
code. Currently Phase I is estimated to be completed by early May.
Our building is 7,500 square feet and all age groups will be welcome. We are a family oriented
business with a designated serving area that only comprises approximately 320 square feet out of
the 7,500. Signage and pony walls will clearly designate our serving area. North of our serving
area we will have a space for minors to order non-alcoholic beverages and refreshments.
Ace’s Range will maintain a “seasonal area” comprising almost 1,000 square feet in which we
will do promotional events. For example, during CFD we may possibly offer the space to a
western boutique vendor, or set up a stage for live music. Ace’s Range will have large screens
for sports television. For the big game, the space could be filled with more seating and tables or
it could be reserved for private parties. Our mission of being a family oriented business will be
demonstrated in our flexibility of that space. Ace’s Range plans to run a fun and engaging
promotional campaign using our point of sale system Tee Snap.
Simulator Technology: Ace’s Range is truly a new and unique experience for Cheyenne. We
will feature six Trackman golf bays and two Smokeless Range shooting simulator bays. Two of
the six Trackman bays will be in a privately enclosed area with their own ambiance separate
from the rest of the building. Trackman is a state-of-the-art golf simulator featuring premiere
courses from all over the world. Trackman is the best medium to teach the game of golf via the
numbers provided for every swing. Smokeless Range is a software that utilizes a laser bullet to
simulate discharging a firearm over various applications. These applications include fast draw,
hostage scenarios, zombies, moving targets and of course standard shooting ranges. Ace’s
Range will host leagues for golf.
Competition: Lastly, with any license, we know you have to consider the potential for
competition with existing businesses and licensees. There are two other known facilities that
have a golf simulator in town. The unique nature of our facility serves a different demand in the
community than the other two businesses. We are not aware of anyone doing virtual shooting
ranges.
We look forward to discussing our project more with you in the coming weeks.
Sincerely,
J Brent “JB” William Kunz
General Manager of Ace’s Range LLC

Ace’s Range Business Concepts and Menu
*Our menu and league schedule is a rough draft and a work in progress. We will most likely be limiting the
ingredients listed below depending on our storage and warming kitchens abilities. Due to our limited kitchen space we
want to offer a convenient and customizable style of various ready to eat and frozen foods that are heated up to
prepare.
Ace’s Range is proud to offer a fully customizable menu that offers unique fare. We have a few set menu items,
however please feel free to create your own culinary delight! We have listed below six categories that you can choose
from to make up your basket. We offer 3 sizes of baskets, The Caddy, Golfer and Greenskeeper. The Caddy is a smaller
portion proportionate to an app or kids serving. The Golfer is a full portion while The Greenskeeper is two baskets.
Due to limited space in our warming kitchen we are proud to offer fare from other Downtown Cheyenne
restaurants for those seeking different options, or those with specific food allergies that conflict with any ingredient on
our list. You may order food from these restaurants while on premise or upon booking a tee time with us, we’ll pick it
up and bring it to you at Ace’s. Please consider a longer wait time for items ordered from other establishments when
ordered while at Ace’s Range, we strongly recommend ordering from other restaurants when you book your tee time to
reduce waiting time. We believe in our local businesses and we’re proud to support them. Thank you for your
patronage, we appreciate your support of local small business!
We will have QR codes in each bay, bar area and at front desk. Patrons will use these QR code to sign into our
system, once you create a login you can create a profile, from which you may book tee times, order food from
Downtown Cheyenne restaurants, register for leagues and tournaments, spend your Ace Bucks and so much more! Our
Ace’s Range membership is starting at $300. This membership includes discounted league and tournament fees, early
booking, event booking, access to recorded lessons anywhere on your mobile device, enrollment in Ace’s Bucks in house
credit system which will include promotions from Cheyenne businesses.
Our normal business hours are subject to change in order meet Cheyenne’s needs, please call or text, (307) 757-
7299 or email us at Acesrangewygmail.com if you’d like to book a private event with catered food service, private
parties including multiple bays, sections of the building, or book a completely private party at Ace’s. We’re open to
personal requests to make your experience special. We do have a set schedule of events we’re excited to offer;
From opening date until leagues start we will have open play with a few tournaments to gauge Cheyenne’s
needs. We encourage the community to sign up for an Ace’s Range membership and establish a handicap (recording
score for 9 holes) used for league and tournament play.
During this opening period we’re excited to offer a unique culinary expertise challenge for patrons who sign up
for an Ace’s Range membership. From opening day for our first month of operation, Our fully certified chef/food
handlers will sample each customers custom Ace’s Range dish entered into the competition. We will record each Ace of
the day with the best recipe. Each Ace will be entered into a private tasting team building event for Ace’s Range
employees. Each Ace will be evaluated by our staff grazing on munchies while enjoying our unique simulator
experience. One winner with the best recipe chosen by Ace’s Range employees will be awarded Ace Bucks. *must sign
up for Ace’s Range membership to qualify.
League Play
12 teams of consisting of 3 players play each other once over 11 weeks. Points are awarded for handicapped
adjusted lvi match play for each team. The Top 4 teams after the group stage make the cut to playoffs, each playoff
contender plays one another for a 13 week season. Prizes from local vendors are to be awarded each week for skins
challenges. Playing the time schedule is encouraged, however players may play the round in advance to accommodate
schedule conflicts. Please notify us and we’ll book you a tee time to record your round for the week.

Open Tee Times/Championship Sunday Once a Month Tournaments
Tournaments
We will host 6-8 Tournaments through out the year, with the majority taking place during colder months. Prizes
will be awarded to the top 4 contestants. A few of our tournaments will be solo competitions ranging over 4 days of
play, most will be team play over 1-2 days depending upon the event.
Open Play
During normal operational business hours we will accept open play if available, we HIGHLY RECOMMEND
booking a tee time in advance at Acesrangewy.com, through our app, or by phone at (307) 757-7299. We rent the bays
by the hour, however in the event of open play consisting of less then one hour we have 15 minute increments for a
quick bucket. Ask about our bucket special!
Lessons are available with state of the art video feedback you can access anywhere with an Ace’s Range
membership.
Get custom fitted clubs suited to you on the best sims on the market.

THE BLUE STEM WINE HOUSE, LIC
1909 WARREN AVENUE
CHEYENNE, WY 62001
307-575-2310
Mayor Patrick Collins
2101 O’Neil Avenue
Cheyenne, WY 82001
November 19, 2022
Dear Mayor Collins and Cheyenne City Council Members:
Please accept this letter of introduction on behalf of The Blue Stem Wine House, LLC (The Blue
Stem) as outlined as a requirement of new liquor license applicants. We sincerely appreciate
the opportunity to share our vision with you.
The Blue Stem is a boutique winery located at 1909 Warren Avenue in a 110-year-old building
currently undergoing extensive renovation by TCI Solutions to ensure the structure complies
with commercial requirements while simultaneously maintaining the integrity of the original
Edwardian Era home. Fermenting will take place in the basement while the main floor will
house a seating area for tasting and charcuterie.
The idea to offer Cheyenne a downtown winery began several years ago and we are nearing the
culmination of this physical, emotional, and financial effort to provide a space for conversation,
small groups, musical entertainment, and those simply seeking a brief respite amidst daily life.
Our vision includes private educational tasting events and wine parings, take out sales, a wine
club, and online orders. Most importantly, however, is our intent to give back to the
community through supporting local charities and offering a space for nurturing philanthropic
endeavors as well as cultivating interpersonal relationships.
We thank you in advance for your support of our interest in creating a unique space in the greater
community of Cheyenne and your endorsement of our winery permit application.
Sincerely,
Erdmann
Brandon Erdmann

STATE OF WYOMING
Office of the Secretary of State
I, EDWARD A. BUCHANAN, Secretary of State of the State of Wyoming, do hereby certify
that the filing requirements for the issuance of this certificate have been fulfilled.
CERTIFICATE OF ORGANIZATION
The Blue Stem Wine House, LLC
I have affixed hereto the Great Seal of the State of Wyoming and duly executed this official
certificate at Cheyenne, Wyoming on this 6th day of November, 2020 at 7:00 PM.
Remainder intentionally left blank.
Filed Date: 11/0612020
Secretary Sr State
Filed Online By:
Virginia Clark
on 11/06/2020
Page 4 of 4

THE BLUE STEM WINE HOUSE, LLC
1909 WARREN AVENUE
CHEYENNE, WY 62001
307-575-2310
October 18, 2022
MEMORANDUM FOR: Cheyenne City Clerk, 2101 O’Neil Ave., Cheyenne, WY 82001
SUBJECT: Proposed Alcohol Training Program
1) This memorandum serves to meet the information requirement concerning a
proposed alcohol training program as identified on the new liquor license applicant
checklist provided by the Cheyenne City Clerk.
2) In accordance with the Wyoming Liquor Division, all Blue Stem owners and
employees will complete one of the approved alcohol served education courses (as
linked in the Wyoming Liquor Division website) prior to serving wine on site.
3) Completion of one of the approved courses is intended to meet the Wyoming
Department of Revenue, Chapter 20 (Liquor Division Methodology), Section 17
requirement of four hours training and remain valid for three years.
3) Certificates of course completion will be maintained on file and made available for
inspection purposes by authorized personnel as outlined in Cheyenne Municipal Code
5.1 2.90 (Ord. No.4186, § 1, 6-26-17).
4) Questions, requests for further information, or clarification concerning the proposed
program may be directed to the undersigned at 307-760-7579 or
brandon(thebluestemwinehouse.com.
Brandon Erdmann
Blue Stem Member

# SOURCE_FILE: CHEY-2023-04-04_TotalExtraction_18319838.pdf
Wyoming
Association of
Municipalities
Building Strong Communities
TO:
All Mayors
FROM:
Earla Checchi, Finance Manager
SUBJECT:
Voting Delegates for the 2023 WAM Summer Convention
DATE:
March 15, 2023
We are requesting that your municipality’s governing body appoint its Official Voting Delegate and
alternate to WAM’s Summer Convention Business Meeting, held Thursday, June 8, 2023, in Cody.
Items that your Voting Delegate will be voting on may include By-law changes, Resolutions,
Membership Dues. Any individual member of the association is entitled to speak during the June
business meeting. However, when a vote is taken on any action the official voting delegate, or the
alternate, is the only one allowed to vote for the city or town. Any elected or appointed official/staff
may be designated by the city/town as its official voting delegate.
Please complete the attached form and email, mail, or fax it to WAM by Friday, May 26, 2023. We
appreciate your cooperation and prompt action on this matter to ensure that each municipality is
represented by a person who has been duly authorized by your governing body to take an active role
in the business meeting.
If you find your official delegate is not able to attend the conference at the last moment, you may re
appoint someone else. For this change to be accepted we do need the change in writing. You may
send/fax it to the WAM office by Friday, May 26 or your voting delegate may bring the written
change/authorization to the convention and submit it to the WAM registration desk by Wednesday,
June 7 before 12:00pm. After that time, changes will not be accepted.
Please contact us with any questions.
Ensure YOUR community has a VOICE and a VOTE at the June business meeting!

WYOMING ASSOCIATION OF
MUNICIPALITIES
2023 WAM SUMMER CONVENTION
OFFICIAL VOTING DELEGATE FORM
The following person has been selected as the Official Voting Delegate for the 2023 WAM Summer
Convention Business Meeting in Cody, Thursday, June 8, 2023.

# SOURCE_FILE: CHEY-2023-02-09_TotalExtraction_28629966.pdf
 
1
RESOLUTION NO. _____________ 
 
Entitled:  
“A RESOLUTION SUPPORTING THE BOARD OF PUBLIC UTILITIES’ 
APPLICATION TO THE WYOMING STATE LOAN AND INVESTMENT 
BOARD FOR A LOCAL GOVERNMENT PROJECT ARPA GRANT FOR 
THE WATER RECLAMATION FACILITIES HYDRAULIC CAPACITY 
PROJECT IN THE AMOUNT OF $1,984,000.00 AND AUTHORIZING THE 
DIRECTOR OF THE BOARD OF PUBLIC UTILITIES TO EXECUTE THE 
APPLICATION AND ALL RELATED DOCUMENTS” 
 
WHEREAS, W.S. § 15-7-401, provides for a Board of Public Utilities (BOPU) to 
manage, operate, maintain, and control municipal waterworks, sanitary sewer system and 
sewage disposal plants; 
 
 
WHEREAS, Cheyenne City Code, Section 2.68.010, establishes that the BOPU has the 
exclusive authority to manage, operate, maintain and control the Cheyenne Municipal 
Waterworks, Sanitary Sewage System and Sewage Disposal Plants of the City and to make all 
rules and regulations necessary for the safe, economical and efficient operation and 
maintenance of the above facilities; 
 
WHEREAS, all sanitary sewer wastewater, including storm water inflow and 
infiltration, flow to the Crow Creek Water Reclamation Facility (WRF) and the Dry Creek WRF 
where the combined flow is treated and discharged to Crow Creek or additionally treated and 
used as recycle water supply; 
 
 
WHEREAS, during storm events, Crow Creek elevation rises and Crow Creek WRF is 
unable to discharge to the creek; 
 
 
WHEREAS, the relocation of the Crow Creek WRF outfall location will allow the plant 
to continue to operate during times of high Crow Creek water elevation; 
 
 
WHEREAS, the 2013 Water and Wastewater Master Plan recommends the installation 
of equalization basins at Dry Creek WRF to store water during high storm flow events and 
allow the plant to treat the water after the storm subsides; 
 
WHEREAS, the BOPU desires to pursue the WRF Hydraulic Capacity Project 
(Project) to install equalization basins at Dry Creek WRF and relocate the outfall pipeline at 
Crow Creek WRF; 
 
WHEREAS, the Local Government Project ARPA Grant program required that certain 
criteria be met, as described in the State Loan and Investment Board’s Rules and Regulations 
governing the program, and to the best of our knowledge the application meets those criteria; 
 
9:48 am, Jan 30 2023

 
2
WHEREAS, the Project cost estimate for engineering, construction, and other 
administrative costs is $3,100,000.00, which includes engineering services, easements, 
construction and miscellaneous costs; 
 
WHEREAS, the Project meets the criteria and is eligible for consideration of grant 
funding from the Water & Sewer ARPA Grant Program in the amount of $1,984,000.00, sixty-
four percent (64%); 
 
WHEREAS, the BOPU is committed to paying the remainder of PROJECT costs from 
revenue from the Sewer Enterprise Fund in the amount of $1,116,000.00, thirty-six percent 
(36%); 
 
WHEREAS, if awarded the ARPA grant, the funds shall only be used to cover costs 
incurred during the period beginning on March 3, 2021 and ending on September 1, 2026; and  
 
 
WHEREAS, the BOPU recognizes that the PROJECT is necessary and that it is 
essential to maintain reliable operation of Dry Creek and Crow Creek WRFs during high storm 
events. 
 
NOW, THEREFORE, BE IT RESOLVED BY THE GOVERNING BODY OF 
THE CITY OF CHEYENNE, that an application be submitted to the Wyoming State Loan 
and Investment Board for a Local Government Project ARPA Grant for an amount not to exceed 
$1,984,000.00 for project costs such as, but not limited to, engineering services, construction, 
administration, and all related costs of the Water Reclamation Facilities Hydraulic Capacity 
Project; 
 
BE IT FURTHER RESOLVED, that the Mayor, the City Clerk, and the Director of 
the Board of Public Utilities are hereby designated as the authorized representatives of the City 
of Cheyenne to act on behalf of the City of Cheyenne in all matters related to this application 
and related documents, including any amendments to the Project Agreement. 
 
PRESENTED, READ AND ADOPTED this ______ day of ________________, 2023. 
 
 
_______________________________________ 
Patrick Collins, Mayor  
 
(SEAL)  
 
 
ATTEST: 
 
____________________________________  
Kristina F. Jones, City Clerk

# SOURCE_FILE: CHEY-2023-02-09_TotalExtraction_55401830.pdf
RECORD OF PROCEEDINGS FOR THE GOVERNING BODY 
OF THE CITY OF CHEYENNE 
Office of City Clerk 
 
February 6, 2023  
 
 
The Governing Body of the City of Cheyenne met in a special session on this date 
beginning at 12:11 p.m. in Committee Room 104 and via electronic conference meeting.  Present 
were: COUNCIL MEMBERS – Dr. Michelle Aldrich, Bryan Cook, Ken Esquibel, Richard 
Johnson, Pete Laybourn, Scott Roybal, Tom Segrave and Jeff White. Absent: MAYOR – Patrick 
Collins and Dr. Mark Rinne. Also present: Kristina F. Jones, City Clerk.   
 
 
RESOLUTION -- Authorizing the submission of an application to the State Loan and 
Investment Board for a grant through the Local Government Project ARPA grant program on 
behalf of the Governing Body for the City of Cheyenne for the purpose of providing critically 
needed flood control at East Dell Range Boulevard and Van Buren Avenue. Reporting for the 
Finance Committee, Mr. White moved to adopt, seconded by Mr. Royal.  Motion carried.  Voting 
“yes” – all members of the governing body present.  Comment was made by the following member 
of the governing body: Mr. Laybourn.  (#6299) 
 
 
RESOLUTION – Supporting the Board of Public Utilities’ application to the Wyoming 
State Loan and Investment Board for a Local Government ARPA Grant for the Water Reclamation 
Facilities (WRF) Hydraulic Capacity Project (Project) in the amount of $1,984,000.00 and 
authorizing the Director of the Board of Public Utilities to execute the application and all related 
documents.  Mr. White advised this item was reviewed under other items for the agenda at the 
February 6, 2023 Finance Committee meeting, therefore, provisions of Title 2 of City Code have 
been met.  Mr. White moved to adopt, seconded by Mr. Esquibel.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” 
– all members of the governing body present.  Comment was made by the following member of 
the governing body: Mr. Laybourn.  (#6300)  
  
 
There being no further business to come before the governing body, the meeting was 
officially adjourned at 12:14 p.m. 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
Submitted by, 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
Kristina F. Jones,    
City Clerk 
 
This notice is available in alternative, accessible formats upon request. 
 
Published: 
Wyoming Tribune-Eagle 
 
 
February 11, 2023

# SOURCE_FILE: CHEY-2023-02-07_TotalExtraction_83462218.pdf
RECORD OF PROCEEDINGS FOR THE GOVERNING BODY 
OF THE CITY OF CHEYENNE 
Office of City Clerk 
 
February 13, 2023  
 
 
The Governing Body of the City of Cheyenne met in regular session on this date beginning 
at 6:00 p.m. in Council Chambers and via electronic conference meeting.  Present were: MAYOR 
– Patrick Collins, COUNCIL MEMBERS – Dr. Michelle Aldrich, Bryan Cook, Ken Esquibel, 
Pete Laybourn, Dr. Mark Rinne, Scott Roybal, Tom Segrave and Jeff White.  Absent: Richard 
Johnson. Also present: Kristina F. Jones, City Clerk.  The pledge of allegiance was recited. 
 
Consent Agenda.  (All agenda items listed with the designation of [CA] are considered to 
be routine items by the governing body and will be enacted by one motion.  There will be no 
separate discussion on these items unless a member of the governing body so requests and support 
by two other members is received.  Any item removed from the Consent Agenda will be considered 
in its normal sequence on the agenda.  Mr. Segrave moved to approve, seconded by Mr. White.  
Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body present. 
 
[CA] 
Minutes from Regular Meeting of the Governing Body on January 23, 2023.  Motion (per 
Consent Agenda) carried.  
 
[CA] 
Minutes from Special Meeting of the Governing Body on February 6, 2023.  Motion (per 
Consent Agenda) carried.  
 
 
Mayor Collins opened a public hearing for a transfer of ownership of retail liquor license 
application filed with the City Clerk’s Office for Wyoming Horse Racing, LLC d/b/a Horse Palace, 
6421 Speer Rd., Cheyenne, WY (license currently held by Poor Richard’s d/b/a Poor Richard’s).  
The purpose of the hearing having been met, Mayor Collins closed the hearing and referred the 
application to Finance Committee. Public comment was made by: Nick Hughes, President of 
Wyoming Horse Racing, LLC.  Comments were made by the following members of the governing 
body: Dr. Aldrich, Mr. Esquibel, and Mr. Laybourn. 
 
 
ORDINANCE –3rd READING – Annexing to the City of Cheyenne, Wyoming, land 
located southwest of the intersection of Ridge Road and Holland Court. Reporting for Public 
Services Committee, Mr. Cook moved to approve on third and final reading, seconded by Mr. 
Segrave.  Mr. Cook moved to postpone to February 27, 2023, seconded by Mr. Segrave. Motion 
to postpone carried. Voting “yes” -- all members of the governing body present.  Public comment 
was made by: Cooper Perryman, AVI, Mathew Kasper, Gateway Construction, Rebecca Murchie, 
and John Pike.  Comments were made by the following members of the governing body: Dr. 
Aldrich, Mayor Collins, and Mr. Cook. 
  
ORDINANCE –3rd READING – Amending the Official Zoning Map of the City of 
Cheyenne changing the zoning classification from County A-1 Agriculture and Rural Residential 
to NR-3 Neighborhood Residential-High Density for land located southwest of the intersection of 
Ridge Road and Holland Court.  Mr. Cook moved to approve on third and final reading, seconded 

by Mr. White. Mr. Cook moved to postpone to February 27, 2023, seconded by Mr. Segrave. 
Motion to postpone carried. Voting “yes” -- all members of the governing body present.  Public 
comment was made by: Josh Applegate. 
 
ORDINANCE –3rd READING – Annexing to the City of Cheyenne, Wyoming, land 
located east of Southwest Drive north of I-80. Mr. Cook moved to approve on third and final

for land located east of Southwest Drive north of I-80.  Mr. Cook moved to approve on third and

ORDINANCE –3rd READING – Vacating West 19th Street, west of Grant Avenue, City of

ORDINANCE –1st READING – Amending Section 1.3 of the Unified Development Code

# SOURCE_FILE: CHEY-2023-02-07_TotalExtraction_77729539.pdf
   
RECORD OF PROCEEDINGS FOR THE GOVERNING BODY 
OF THE CITY OF CHEYENNE 
Office of City Clerk 
 
February 16, 2023  
 
 
The Governing Body of the City of Cheyenne met in special session on this date beginning 
at 6:00 p.m. in Committee Room 104, 2101 O’Neil Ave, and via electronic conference meeting, 
for the purpose of collective labor bargaining with the Cheyenne Professional Firefighters, 
International Association of Firefighters, Local No. 279.  Present were: MAYOR – Patrick Collins, 
COUNCIL MEMBERS – Dr. Michelle Aldrich, Bryan Cook (arriving at 6:34 p.m. during 
executive session), Ken Esquibel, Richard Johnson, Pete Laybourn, Dr. Mark Rinne, Scott Roybal, 
Tom Segrave and Jeff White.  Also present: Kristina F. Jones, City Clerk; Stefanie Boster, City 
Attorney; Jo Jordan, Legal Assistant, City Attorney’s Office; Robin Lockman, City Treasurer, and 
Chief John Kopper, Cheyenne Fire and Rescue.  The pledge of allegiance was recited. 
 
Mayor Collins advised the meeting would be audio recorded and available on Facebook 
live and stated that Wyoming State Statute §16-4-405(a)(x) of the Public Meetings Act allows the 
governing body to meet in executive session to consider or tender offers regarding salaries, wages 
or other terms and conditions of employment during negotiations with the firefighters union and 
that any motion approved by the governing body to move into executive session during the special 
meetings would be pursuant to this section of statute.    
 
Mayor Collins provided opening comments noting he has been meeting with the Fire 
Department for labor management discussions which have resulted in a better understanding of 
the functions of their department.  Mayor Collins expressed appreciation to Chief John Kopper for 
the work he does with the Union and his representation of the Fire Department and to members of 
the Union’s negotiation team.  The proposed Ground Rules were agreed upon and signed by the 
City and Union. [Clerk’s note: City’s Spokespersons are: Mayor Patrick Collins, City Attorney 
Stefanie Boster and City Council President Richard Johnson.  Union’s Spokespersons are: Neil 
Durante, Vincent McQueeney and Kelsey Raile.]  
 
Following signing of the Ground Rules by Mayor Collins and Union Spokesperson Vincent 
McQueeney both parties presented their respective proposed Collective Bargaining Agreement 
proposals.  Mayor Collins briefly reviewed the City’s proposal which included changes to the 
following areas and Sections: update cover page; update table of contents; Section 2-Definitions; 
Section 3-Exclusive Bargaining Agent; Section 4-Term of Agreement; Section 8-Grievance 
Procedures; Section 12-Union Representation; Section 13-Management Rights; Section 15-
Department Staffing; Section 19-Inclement Weather; Section 21-Classification of Employment 
and Basic Wages; Section 22-Overtime; Section 23-Working in a Higher Job Rank; Section 24-
Conversion of Benefits; Section 26-Vacation/EOD; Section 31-Uniforms; Section 32-Personal 
Protective Equipment; and Section 42-Longevity.  Mr. McQueeney briefly reviewed the Union’s 
proposal which included changes to the following areas and Sections: update cover page; update 
table of contents; Section 2-Definitions, Section 15-Department Staffing; Section 20-Holidays; 

   
Section 21-Classification of Employment and Basic Wages; Section 22-Overtime; Section 23-
Working in a Higher Job Rank; and Section 26-Vacation/EOD  
 
Mr. Johnson moved to meet in executive session, seconded by Mr. Roybal.  Motion carried.  
Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body present.  Those present in executive session 
were:  All members of the governing body; Stefanie Boster, City Attorney; Kristina F. Jones, City 
Clerk; Robin Lockman, City Treasurer; Chief John Kopper, Cheyenne Fire & Rescue; and Jo 
Jordan, City Attorney Legal Assistant.  [Clerk’s Note: executive session convened at 6:27 p.m.] 
 
 
Following closure of the executive session, Mayor Collins reconvened the regular meeting 
at 7:02 p.m.  Union Spokesperson Mr. McQueeney echoed the importance of the labor 
management discussions that occur throughout the year leading up to negotiations.  Mr. 
McQueeney announced the Union’s acceptance of the City’s proposed agreement as written.  
Mayor Collins announced the City’s acceptance of the Union’s proposed agreement as written.  
 
  
There being no further business to come before the governing body, the meeting was 
officially adjourned at 7:05 p.m. 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
Submitted by, 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
Kristina F. Jones, City Clerk  
 
 
This notice is available in alternative, accessible formats upon request. 
 
Published: 
Wyoming Tribune-Eagle 
 
 
February 25, 2023  
 
 
 
 
 


--- END OF FILE ---

Seth Lloyd

UDC-22-00425-01 | 5908 Ridge Road
Annexation
Date:
January 9, 2023
Background: 
The item for consideration is an Annexation.  This is approved by Ordinance.   
The proposed Governing Body review schedule is as follows:
Introduction and Referral 
1/9/2023
Public Services Committee 
1/17/2023
Second reading 
1/23/2023
Public Services Committee 
2/7/2023
Third and final reading 
2/13/2023

Planning and Development Department
